Let's look at state-of-art LP approach. It uses f nonnegative normalized sum sqrt(2n), M=||f*f||∞. Solve LP maximize sum_j b_j subject to b_j≥0 and ||f*g||∞≤M. Since f*g <= M for all k. For fixed f, to maximize total mass of g under cross-correlation nonnegative box constraint [0,M]. This is a linear program: variables g_j, constraints a*k: (f*g)(k)=∑ f_{k-j}g_j ≤ M for k=0..2n-2, g_j≥0. Objective sum g. Then rescale and convex update. This is like finding the maximal nonnegative sequence under the same peak with f as kernel. If f is near optimal, this LP yields improvement.

LP formulation: Given f (nonnegative sum=1), M = max(f*f) (we can enforce maybe slight slack). Maximize 1^T g subject to A g ≤ M*ones, g≥0, where A is convolution matrix (2n-1 x n): A_{k,j} = f_{k-j} if 0<=k-j<n else 0. Since f nonnegative. The base max for uniform is 1/n. If M < average? We can run LP iterations:
```
def lp_iterate(a, time_limit, max_iters, slack=1e-6, mix_choices):
  best=a,best_obj
  f=normalize(a); conv=obj...
  for it in range(max_iters):
    M=conv.max()
    g=lp_update(f,M*(1+1e-7))
    if g=None: break
    candidates=[g]
    for t in [0.5,0.2,0.8,1.0]:
       c=(1-t)f+t*g; c=normalize(c)
    j=score candidates pick best
    if best improves: f=best_candidate; best_obj=...
    else: # try smaller t or slack? reduce? break?
  return best
```

Given f normalized sum=sqrt(2n)? In our normalized sum=1. Let M = max(f*f). LP: maximize ∑g subject to (f*g)≤M, g≥0. Let optimal sum s. Then g'=g/s. The cross peak (f*g')≤M/s. If s>1, cross peak lower. Does this imply self peak lower for mixture? They claim yes with small t. Direct g may not; but iterative works. If s huge, g may be weird. We can use mix.
Could add constraint ∑g=1? Then maximize min slack? Not. The previous LP maximizes sum under same M, ensuring feasible set nonempty (f). The scalar s measures how much mass can be added while keeping cross-correlation under M. For uniform, s=1 (due full window). For nonuniform, s likely >1; g' is rescaled with lower cross peaks.

We can compute `M_f = max_conv(f)`. If f sum=1. The score S = 2n M_f. If we get g' with score lower. We can test in code.

Pseudo:
```
def lp_iterate(a0, time_limit, max_iters=100, mix_choices=(0.2,0.5,0.8)):
    a = normalize(a0)
    conv = convolve_full(a); obj = 2*n*conv.max()
    best_a=a; best_obj=obj
    t0=time.monotonic()
    stall=0
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time > limit: break
       M = conv.max()
       g = lp_update(a, M*(1+1e-7))
       if g is None: break
       # candidate best
       best_cand = a; best_cand_obj=obj
       for mm in mix_choices:
          c=(1-mm)*a + mm*g; c=normalize(c)
          objc = score_conv(c)
          if objc < best_cand_obj: cand=c
       # also g normalized itself (mm=1)
       if best_cand is a and it? maybe
       if best_cand_obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
           a=best_cand; conv=...; obj=best_obj; best...; stall=0
       else:
           # try direct g with smaller slack? Or mix 0.1
           c=0.9*a+0.1*g; ... maybe
           if improves: ...
           else:
              stall+=1
              if stall>5: break
    return best_a,best_obj
```

   F = rfft(f,L)

O. Coordinate descent by adjusting two coordinates with golden:
Maybe implement after gradient/LP as final polish. It can reduce score by 0.1%. Let's design simpler guarantee:
- Use current best a (sum=1), conv via FFT.

Implement O(n) move carefully. We need `apply_move_inplace(conv, a, p, q, delta, n)` modifies conv using original a. But if we modify a after, okay. Function:
```
def apply_move_inplace(conv, a, p, q, delta):
    if delta==0 or p==q: return
    n=len(a)
    # linear based on original a
    conv[p:p+n] -= 2.0*delta*a
    conv[q:q+n] += 2.0*delta*a
    # quadratic
    conv[p+p] += delta*delta
    conv[q+q] += delta*delta
    if p != q:
        conv[p+q] -= 2.0*delta*delta
    # Note if p+q maybe out of range? p,q<n => sum ≤2n-2 valid.
```
This uses a original for linear terms. If p and q slices overlap, adding both with original a is correct because linear terms from both deltas simultaneous. However if p+q overlaps with p+p or q+q? The formula addition of point terms regardless of overlaps: The total quadratic coefficient Q_k = δ²(1_{k=2p}+1_{k=2q}-2 1_{k=p+q}) (if p≠q). If e.g., 2p=p+q (p=q) not; if 2p=2q no. Distinct. Good.
But wait if p+q index lies within both linear slices, we added linear terms and then point quadratic. Total C_k = old + 2δ(a_{k-q}-a_{k-p}) + δ²Q_k. Correct.
Now if we apply remove slice then add slice, and p/q slices overlap, both use same base a. Good.
Need ensure conv length: p:p+n end p+n <=2n-1 since p≤n-1. q likewise.
After move, update a[p]-=delta, a[q]+=delta. If due floating a[p] negative small, clip 0 and adjust? We choose δ≤a[p]*(1-eps). If p chosen source a[p]>0.
After many moves, conv drift due rounding; recompute via FFT every e.g. 1000 accepted moves or 1 sec.

To evaluate candidate move without copying conv: We can temporarily apply to conv, compute max, then revert if not accept. To revert, apply inverse move (-delta) on conv and a. But if we update a then revert. Simpler: copy conv and a p/q? Since conv length 2n-1, copying cheap. For golden, many evaluations; maybe use in-place with revert for speed? Copy in C maybe fast. We'll implement `evaluate_move(convm, a, p,q,delta)` that copies conv? If in golden many copies, memory allocation overhead. Could implement `move_score(conv, a,p,q,delta)` using np operations on slices without modifying conv: compute candidate values in the union of intervals. But allocating candidate array length up to 2n anyway. Copy is fine for n=256. For speed, maybe not use golden; use a few δ and in-place with revert.

Local polish algorithm:
```
def local_polish(a0, time_limit, rng, max_steps=100000):
   a=normalize; conv=fft; obj=...
   best...
   recompute_counter=0
   while time<limit:
      # compute active peaks: top indices (maybe 8)
      top = argpartition(-conv, topk)[:topk] sorted? weights = conv[top]/sum
      # source_score vector:
      sg = active_subgradient(conv,a,temp? top peaks only) maybe computed loop over top peaks
      source_weight = np.maximum(sg,0)*a
      source_total = source_weight.sum()
      if source_total<...: source_weight = np.maximum(a,1e-9)
      # choose p with prob source_weight/source_total, but favor top few
      p = rng.choice(n,p=source_weight/source_total)
      # target weight: inverse of sg? low source, maybe plus (1-a)
      target_w = 1.0 + np.maximum(-sg,0)*? or 1/(1+sg)
      target_w[p]=0
      q = rng.choice(n,p=target_w/target_w.sum())
      # choose delta: try fractions [0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0?]*a[p] but limit to not overshoot objective
      best_delta=0; best_new=obj
      for frac in fractions:
         d=frac*a[p]
         # evaluate by copy
         trial=conv.copy()
         apply_move_inplace(trial,a,p,q,d)
         newmax=trial.max()
         newobj=2*n*newmax
         if newobj<best_new: ...
      if best_delta>0:
         apply to conv,a; obj=best_new; record; recompute_counter++
      else:
         # maybe random p/q or increase temperature: accept random move if small worsen? 
         # Simulated annealing T = T0*(1-frac)^2
         d = a[p]*rng.uniform(0.05,0.5)
         evaluate; delta_obj = newobj-obj
         if delta_obj<0 or random<exp(-delta_obj/T): accept (apply)
         obj/new? after accept, if best improve record.
      if recompute_counter>500: recompute conv=fft; conv_max; obj=2n convmax; recompute_counter=0
   return best
```
This can get stuck; use multiple temperatures. For hill climb, once no improvement, anneal random moves. But random moves likely worsen; may wander. Use local subgradient to guide.

R. We can implement a direct "peak reduction" by solving convex problem:
Minimize ∑_k max(0, C_k - M)^p for decreasing M? This is smooth. Use gradient descent on a (simplex) with subgradient of excess. We can binary search M? The previous state-of-art uses M. We can use penalty: For target T, minimize `∑ exp(β C_k)` and then lower T. This is what mirror/PGD does with softmax. We can improve by using exact active set: at optimum, we can identify lags where C_k=M. We can solve a linear system to equalize active peaks by adjusting sum? Similar to water-filling: Suppose active set K. We want C_k = M for k∈K, sum a=1, a≥0. This is  |K|+1 equations. Could solve with Lagrange: gradient zero: ∑_{k∈K} λ_k ∂C_k/∂a_i + μ + ν_i =0 on support. Hard.

But we can do "combine" of active peaks: For current active top peaks, compute a "flatening" direction by solving min t subject to linearized C_k ≤ t and sum=1,a≥0. We can add variables a (not LP?) Actually linearize C_k at a: C_k ≈ D_k + G_k . This is a linear program in a! Let's formulate: min z s.t. G_k a ≤ D_k + z for all k, sum a=1,a≥0. Here G_k is row of gradient? ∂C_k/∂a_i = a_{k-i} (using current a). This LP is in a (variables n) with 2n-1 inequalities. It directly chooses a that minimizes the linearized max. This is similar to the state-of-art LP but with objective z instead of maximize sum g, and uses current a in both G and D. The variable is not g but next a. It guarantees first-order descent if we take small step? It may jump far. We can mix. This LP is maybe more targeted than maximizing sum g. It includes constant D_k, so z can be negative. Let's derive:
At current a, for any candidate b, linearized C(b)≈C(a)+∇C(a; a->b)?? ∂C(a)/∂a_i =? If treat variables b, grad at a for b direction? For C_k(b) = b^T P_k b. Linearization around a: C_k(b) ≈ C_k(a) + 2 a^T P_k (b-a). In components: D_k + 2 ∑_{i} a_{k-i} (b_i) - 2 D_k = 2 ∑ a_{k-i} b_i - D_k. So constraint: 2 (A_r? f=b with kernel a) ≤ D_k + z. Equiv (a*b)_k ≤ (D_k+z)/2. We can solve LP:
min z
s.t. for each k: ∑_i A_{k,i} b_i ≤ (D_k + z)/2
∑ b_i =1, b≥0.
A_{k,i}=a_{k-i} if valid.
This is linear in b and z! We can convert z variable free. For linprog, objective minimize z. Inequalities: A b - 0.5 z ≤ D/2. z free? Bound z lower -maxD maybe. We can add b0 lower. Since b sum1, objective bounded. This LP might produce a strong "convex inner approximation" step. It directly minimizes linearized peak, not maximize mass. It might be much better than previous LP! Let's test uniform: D_k triangular. A=uniform. Constraints (1/n)∑ window ≤ (D_k+z)/2. For center D=n? With sum1, center D=1/n. z maybe D -? The solution likely uniform, z=D. `normalize_sequence`:
```
def normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    return a/s
```
If empty, return empty? propose ensures nonempty. For safety, if size==0: return np.array([1.0]).

`convolve_full`:
```
def convolve_full(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.zeros(0)
    L=get_fft_size(2*n-1)
    af=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(af*af, L)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    # clean tiny negative
    if c.size:
       # Convolution nonnegative but FFT roundoff may neg tiny
       c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)  # is this safe? Objective max could be affected by tiny negative? Max positive. Clipping negative to 0 okay. But if all negative due error? no.
    return c
```
Clipping negative to 0 might slightly underestimate max if true small negative? Conv nonnegative; true negative impossible. Good.
But for local move updates, we maintain conv; may have tiny negative due rounding. Max okay.

`get_fft_size`: Use dict cache. Need top-level `FFT_CACHE = {}`
```
def get_fft_size(m):
    try: return FFT_CACHE[m]
    except KeyError:
       p=1
       while p<m: p <<=1
       FFT_CACHE[m]=p
       return p
```

`sequence_score`: 
```
def sequence_score_normalized(a):
    a=normalize_sequence(a); n=a.size; if n==0 return inf
    conv=convolve_full(a); mx=float(conv.max()) if conv.size else 0.0
    return 2.0*n*mx
```
Maybe compute directly for candidate `score_from_conv(a, conv)`.

`active_subgradient`:
Need choose top_k. `top_k` argument. If `top_k > conv.size`, top_k=conv.size. If top_k <=0, top_k=1. Use `np.argpartition` but if top_k == size, no need. For weights, to avoid zero if max zero. 
```
def active_subgradient(conv, a, top_k=32):
    n=a.size; m=conv.size
    if m ==0: return zeros
    kpick = min(max(1,int(top_k)), m)
    if kpick >= m:
       idx=np.arange(m); w=conv.copy(); w=np.maximum(w,0); sw=w.sum(); if sw<1e-300: w=np.ones(m)/m else w/=sw
    else:
       # use argpartition, then sort maybe not needed
       idx = np.argpartition(-conv, kpick-1)[:kpick]
       # ensure include max? argpartition includes but maybe not first; fine.
       w = conv[idx]
       w = np.maximum(w,0.0)
       sw=w.sum()
       if sw < 1e-300:
          w=np.ones(kpick)/kpick
       else:
          # emphasize largest by square?
          # w = w / sw maybe; or w = (w-w.min())+eps
          w = w / sw
    sg=np.zeros(n)
    # for numerical, process descending weights? loop over all
    for ii in range(len(idx)):
       k=int(idx[ii]); w=float(w[ii])
       if w <=0: continue
       lo = 0 if k < n else k - n + 1  # k-(n-1)
       hi = n-1 if k > n-1 else k
       if lo > hi: continue
       start = k - lo
       stop = k - hi - 1  # exclusive; if stop == -1, slice to beginning
       # Need slice a[start:stop:-1] but if stop <0 works only if stop is -1. In Python, start:stop:-1 with stop=-1 stops at index 0. If stop computed less than -1? Let's test: k=n, lo=1, hi=n-1, stop = n-(n-1)-1=0. a[n-1:0:-1] gives n-1..1, correct. lo=0,hi=k<n: stop=-1. Good. For k=0: lo=0,hi=0,start=0,stop=-1. Good. For k=2n-2: lo=n-1,hi=n-1,start=n-1,stop=-1. Good. So stop can be 0 or -1.
       seg = a[start:stop:-1]
       # Is length hi-lo+1? If stop=0 and start=n-1 length n-1? indices n-1..1 yes. If stop=-1 length start+1. Good.
       sg[lo:hi+1] += w * seg
    sg -= np.mean(sg)
    return sg
```
Potential slice bug: When stop = -1, Python `a[5:-1:-1]` gives elements 5,4,3,2,1,0. Good. When stop=0, `a[5:0:-1]` gives 5,4,3,2,1 (stops before 0), length 5. For k=n? n=3, k=3? max k=4. Example n=3,k=3: lo=1,hi=2,start=2, stop=3-2-1=0, a[2:0:-1] -> index2,1 correct. Good.
If hi=n-1 and k= n-1? lo=0,hi=n-1,start=n-1, stop=-1, length n. good.

`mirror_descent` implementation details:
```
def mirror_descent(a0, time_limit, t_init=40.0, eta_init=0.35, top_k=32):
    a=normalize_sequence(a0); n=a.size
    conv=convolve_full(a); obj=2*n*conv.max(); best=a.copy(); best_obj=obj
    eta=eta_init; t=t_init # t unused if top_k
    patience=0; max_pat=30; count=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-start < time_limit:
       sg=active_subgradient(conv,a,top_k)
       scale=sg.max()-sg.min()
       if scale<1e-18: break
       sg=sg/scale  # normalize gradient to O(1)
       logf = -eta*sg
       logf = np.clip(logf, -50, 50)
       logf -= logf.max()
       newa = a * np.exp(logf)
       newa = np.maximum(newa, 0.0)
       s=newa.sum()
       if s<1e-15: break
       newa /= s
       newconv=convolve_full(newa); newobj=2*n*newconv.max()
       # optional accept if not too worse? For nonconvex, always take? It may oscillate. Use accept if newobj <= obj*(1+0.01) or random?
       if newobj <= obj + 1e-12:
          a=newa; conv=newconv; obj=newobj
       else:
          # try smaller eta
          # accept best among eta/2, eta/4?
          ...
       if obj < best_obj - 1e-12: best...
       patience++...
       if patience>max_pat: eta*=0.7; ...
       count++
    return best, best_obj
```
Since subgradient normalized, eta can be moderate. Alternative use top_k weights always scale; eta 0.1-1. We can implement backtracking:
- Try multipliers = [1, 0.5, 0.25] * eta. Compute newobj for each? Expensive but small. We can do only if no improve for a few. Simpler adaptive.
- To prevent accepting worsening causing escape from local but diverging, use condition `newobj <= obj * (1 + 0.005) + 1e-12`; else halve eta and try once. Implementation:
```
improved_step=False
for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25):
   logf = -eta*mult*sgnorm
   ... newobj
   if newobj <= obj + 1e-10:
      accept; break
if not accept: eta*=0.5; continue
```
This is okay. Since normalized sg, objective changes maybe small. Starting eta 1.0? Let's set eta_init=0.8, min 1e-4. `sgnorm = sg - sg.mean(); scale = max(abs(sgnorm).max(), 1e-18); sgnorm/=scale`. But scale ~? okay.
Use `t` not needed; top_k fixed. Maybe top_k decreases over iterations: top_k = max(8, int(32*(1-2*patience?)))? We'll keep constant per call with parameter.

LP functions:
Need import linprog. If not available, functions return None.
Build A efficiently:
```
def build_conv_matrix(a):
    n=len(a); A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n))
    for j in range(n):
       A[j:j+n,j] = a
    return A
```
This loops n; for n=512, 512 slices, okay. Could vectorize with strides but okay.
`lp_max_sum_update(f, M, time_limit_seconds=15)`:
```
def lp_max_sum_update(f, M, time_limit_seconds=15.0):
    if linprog is None: return None
    f=normalize_sequence(f); n=f.size
    if n>768: return None # maybe
    A=build_conv_matrix(f)
    b=np.full(2*n-1, M, dtype=np.float64)
    c=-np.ones(n)
    bounds=[(0.0,None) for _ in range(n)]
    try:
       res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'time_limit': max(1.0, time_limit_seconds)})
    except Exception:
       return None
    if res is None or not res.success:
       # status 1? Maybe unbounded? return None; if res.x finite maybe use
       if hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)) and res.x.sum()>0:
           g=res.x
       else: return None
    else:
       g=res.x
    g=np.maximum(np.asarray(g,dtype=np.float64),0)
    s=g.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return None
    return g/s
```
If method high default? If scipy version no high, use `method='highs'` may error; catch and try default? Could use `linprog(..., method='highs')` inside try; except: try `method=None`. But options time_limit maybe unknown for other methods. Simpler: if `linprog` available, call with method='highs'; if TypeError, call default without options.
```
try:
   res=linprog(..., method='highs', options={'time_limit': tl})
except Exception:
   try: res=linprog(..., options={'time_limit': tl})
   except: res=linprog(...)
```
But top-level lengthy. We'll implement helper `run_linprog(c,A,b,bounds,equal,None, time_limit)`.
`linprog` supports `A_eq`, `b_eq`.
For linearized update:
```
def lp_linearized_update(a, time_limit_seconds=15.0):
   if linprog is None: return None
   a=normalize; n; if n>768: return None
   conv=convolve_full(a); D=conv; Dmax=D.max(); if Dmax<... return a
   A=build_conv_matrix(a)
   rhs=0.5*D
   # matrix with extra z column: A_aug = [A | -0.5]
   # In linprog, add column to A: shape (m,n+1)
   m=A.shape[0]
   Aaug = np.zeros((m,n+1))
   Aaug[:,:n]=A
   Aaug[:,n] = -0.5
   c=np.zeros(n+1); c[n]=1.0
   lb= -Dmax*2 - 1e-6; ub= Dmax*2 + 1
   bounds = [(0,None)]*n + [(lb,ub)]
   res=run_linprog(c, Aaug, rhs, bounds, None, None, tl)
   if fail: return None
   b=res.x[:n]; clip normalize; return b
```

`run_linprog` top-level:
```
def run_linprog(c, A_ub, b_ub, bounds, A_eq=None, b_eq=None, time_limit=15.0):
    if linprog is None: return None
    try:
       return linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, method='highs', options={'time_limit': max(1.0, float(time_limit))})
    except Exception:
       try:
          return linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq)
       except Exception:
          return None
```
If A_eq None, okay.

def lp_improve_sequence(a0, time_limit, max_iters=200, use_linear=True, use_maxsum=True, mix_values=(0.25,0.5,0.8,1.0), lp_time=10.0):
   a=normalize; n; conv,obj best
   start=time.monotonic(); stall=0
   while time - start < time_limit:
       M=conv.max()
       candidates=[]
       if use_linear:
           b=lp_linearized_update(a, lp_time)
           if b is not None: candidates.append(b)
       if use_maxsum:
           g=lp_max_sum_update(a, M*(1+1e-8), lp_time)
           if g is not None: candidates.append(g)
       best_step=None; best_step_obj=obj
       for base in candidates:
           for mm in mix_values:
               if mm >=1: cand=base.copy()
               else: cand=(1-mm)*a + mm*base
               cand=normalize(cand)
               c=convolve_full(cand); o=2*n*c.max()
               if o < best_step_obj:
                   best_step_obj=o; best_step=c
       if best_step is None:
           # maybe no LP available or no improvement; break after few
           stall +=1
           if stall>3: break
           continue
       a=best_step; conv=convolve_full(a); obj=best_step_obj
       if obj < best -...:
          best=...; stall=0
       else:
          # step was not better than current? Actually best_step_obj should be < current? We set best_step_obj initialized obj, so only if improved. If no improvement, stall.
          stall +=1
          if stall>5: break
       # optional slow LP time reduce? 
   return best,best_obj
```

`def fourier_phase_init(n, start_mode, steps, rng):`
- start = if 'uniform' ones/n; else maybe U-shape? We'll pass.

`normalize_sequence`: If input list, convert. If scalar? no.
```
def normalize_sequence(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1:
       arr = arr.ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
       return np.ones(1)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
       return np.ones(arr.size) / float(arr.size)
    arr = arr / s
    return arr
```
No inf/nan? If contains nan, sum nan. We should sanitize: `arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)` then clip. Use `np.isfinite`.
```
arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
```

```
c = np.fft.irfft(hf, L).real
c = c[:2*n-1]
c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
return c

def sequence_score(a):
   a=normalize_sequence(a); n=a.size
   if n==0: return inf
   c=convolve_full(a); return 2.0*n*float(c.max()) if c.size else inf
```

`project_simplex` from previous, but use n=1 case.
```
def project_simplex(v):
   v=np.asarray(v,float); n=v.size
   if n==0: return v
   if n==1: return np.ones(1) # sum 1? Actually projection of [x] onto sum1 => [1]. yes.
   u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
   cssv=np.cumsum(u)
   cond=u*np.arange(1,n+1) > (cssv-1.0)
   rho=np.nonzero(cond)[0]
   if rho.size==0: return np.ones(n)/n
   rho=rho[-1]
   theta=(cssv[rho]-1.0)/(rho+1)
   return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```

`active_subgradient`: Need top_k as int maybe based on n. Ensure conv and a same. Use `idx` and `weights`. Potential if conv all zeros. We'll implement top_k default 24.
```
def active_subgradient(conv, a, top_k=32):
   n=int(a.size); m=int(conv.size)
   sg=np.zeros(n)
   if m==0 or n==0: return sg
   kpick=int(top_k)
   if kpick < 1: kpick=1
   if kpick > m: kpick=m
   if kpick == m:
       idx=np.arange(m)
       w=np.maximum(conv,0.0)
       sw=float(w.sum())
       if sw < 1e-300: w=np.ones(m)/m
       else: w/=sw
   else:
       idx=np.argpartition(-conv, kpick-1)[:kpick]
       # include max explicitly? argpartition should include but if top_k small and max maybe in rest? It guarantees at most? Actually argpartition puts kpick elements smallest? `np.argpartition(-conv,kpick-1)[:kpick]` gets kpick largest but not sorted. It includes max. Good.
       w=np.maximum(conv[idx],0.0)
       sw=float(w.sum())
       if sw <1e-300: w=np.ones(kpick)/kpick
       else:
          # use squared to emphasize? Normalize after square
          w2 = w*w
          s2=float(w2.sum())
          if s2 > 1e-300: w=w2/s2
          else: w=w/sw
   for i in range(kpick):
      k=int(idx[i]); wi=float(w[i])
      if wi <=0: continue
      lo = k - n + 1
      if lo < 0: lo = 0
      hi = k
      if hi >= n: hi = n-1
      if lo > hi: continue
      start = k - lo
      stop = k - hi - 1
      # Python slice with step -1. If stop < 0, it works as -1 if stop == -1. If stop could be -2? Let's ensure stop never < -1. min stop = -1 when hi=k. if hi=n-1, stop=k-n. For k<n-1 stop negative? but hi=k then stop=-1. For k>=n-1, hi=n-1, stop=k-n. k min n-1 => stop=-1; k max 2n-2 => stop=n-2? Wait if hi=n-1, stop=k-(n-1)-1=k-n. For k=2n-2 stop=n-2. But lo=n-1, start=n-1. Slice a[n-1:n-2:-1] gives index n-1 only? Stop n-2, yes length1. Good. So stop >= -1.
      seg = a[start:stop:-1]
      sg[lo:hi+1] += wi * seg
   sg -= np.mean(sg)
   return sg
```
Test n=3,k=2 (center? max 4). lo=0? k-n+1=0, hi=2, start=2, stop= -1? k=2, hi=2 => stop=-1. seg a[2:-1:-1]=a2,a1,a0. Good.
k=3: lo=1,hi=2,start=2, stop=3-2-1=0. a[2:0:-1]=a2,a1 length2. Good.

`mirror_descent` with normalized sg:
```
def mirror_descent(a0, time_limit, eta_init=0.8, top_k=32, max_pat=40):
    a=normalize_sequence(a0); n=a.size; conv=convolve_full(a); obj=2*n*conv.max(); best=a.copy(); best_obj=obj
    eta=eta_init; patience=0; start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic()-start < time_limit and time_limit>0:
       sg=active_subgradient(conv,a,top_k)
       sgn = sg - np.mean(sg)
       scale=float(np.abs(sgn).max())
       if scale < 1e-18: break
       sgn /= scale
       accepted=False
       # try multipliers [1,0.5,0.25]
       for mult in (1.0,0.5,0.25):
          lf = -eta*mult*sgn
          lf = np.clip(lf, -50, 50)
          lf -= lf.max()
          na = a * np.exp(lf)
          na = np.maximum(na,0)
          ss=na.sum()
          if ss < 1e-15: break
          na /= ss
          nc=convolve_full(na); no=2*n*nc.max()
          if no <= obj + 1e-12:
              a=na; conv=nc; obj=no; accepted=True; break
       if not accepted:
          eta *= 0.5
          patience += 1
          if patience >= max_pat:
             patience=0
             eta = max(0.05, eta_init*0.5)
          # if obj still best? 
       else:
          patience = 0 if obj < best_obj else min(patience+1, max_pat)
       if obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
          best_obj=obj; best=a.copy()
       # if time almost
    return best, best_obj
```
Issue: If no accepted, we don't update a; just adjust eta. Could stall. If no accepted after several, maybe accept the best candidate among mult even if slightly worse to escape? For nonconvex, sometimes. We can after multipliers compute `best_trial` and if not accepted and patience>10, accept trial with smallest obj (even worse) with small probability? Keep simple: always accept the best trial (even if slightly worse) but update patience if worse than current. This can explore. Let's modify:
- Keep `best_trial_a`, `best_trial_obj` among mult; accept if best_trial_obj <= obj*(1+0.02) maybe, else reject. If no reject for 10, accept best_trial to move. But can drift. We can just require <= obj + 1e-8*max(1,obj)? If none, reduce eta. If still none, accept best trial if patience>15.
Implement enough.

def local_polish(a0, time_limit, rng=None):
    if rng is None: rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)
    a=normalize_sequence(a0); n=a.size
    conv=convolve_full(a); obj=2*n*conv.max()
    best=a.copy(); best_obj=obj
    start=time.monotonic(); recompute=0; no_improve=0
    top_k=max(8, min(32, n//8))
    while time.monotonic()-start < time_limit:
       frac = min(1.0,(time.monotonic()-start)/max(time_limit,1.0))
       T = 2e-4 * (1.0-frac)**2 + 1e-6
       sg=active_subgradient(conv,a,top_k)
       src=np.maximum(sg,0.0)*a + 1e-12
       ssum=src.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(ssum) or ssum<1e-15: src=np.maximum(a,0.0); ssum=src.sum(); if ssum<... src=np.ones(n)/n
       else: src=src/ssum
       tgt=1.0 + 2.0*np.maximum(-sg,0.0)
       # target proportional to inverse source? also avoid p later
       p=int(rng.choice(n, p=src))
       if a[p] <= 1e-15:
           continue
       tgt[p]=0.0
       tsum=tgt.sum()
       if tsum<1e-15:
           tgt=np.ones(n); tgt[p]=0; tsum=n-1
       else: tgt=tgt/tsum
       q=int(rng.choice(n, p=tgt))
       base=a[p]
       best_delta=0.0; best_new=obj
       for frac_move in (0.25,0.5,1.0):
           d=base*frac_move
           trial=conv.copy()
           apply_move_inplace(trial,a,p,q,d)
           nm=float(trial.max())
           no=2.0*n*nm
           if no < best_new:
              best_new=no; best_delta=d
       # Also maybe golden? Skip.
       if best_delta > 0.0:
           apply_move_inplace(conv,a,p,q,best_delta)
           a[p] = max(0.0,a[p]-best_delta)
           a[q] += best_delta
           obj=best_new
           recompute +=1
           if obj < best_obj:
               best_obj=obj; best=a.copy(); no_improve=0
           else:
               no_improve +=1
       else:
           # try annealed random small move, maybe with random p/q instead of guided?
           d=base*rng.uniform(0.05,0.35)
           trial=conv.copy(); apply_move_inplace(trial,a,p,q,d); no=2nmax
           delta_obj=no-obj
           accept=False
           if delta_obj <= 1e-12: accept=True
           elif rng.random() < math.exp(-delta_obj/max(T,1e-12)): accept=True
           if accept:
              apply_move_inplace(conv,a,p,q,d); a[p]=max(0,a[p]-d); a[q]+=d; obj=no; recompute+=1
              if obj<best_obj: ...
           else:
              no_improve +=1
       if recompute > 300:
           conv=convolve_full(a); obj=2*n*float(conv.max()); recompute=0
       if no_improve > 2000:
           # smooth reset: a = normalize(0.95*a + 0.05*uniform) maybe; reset conv
           a = normalize_sequence(a + 0.05*(np.ones(n)/n - a?)?) 
```

def build_conv_matrix_plus(zcol=-0.5):
   n=len(a); m=2*n-1; A=np.zeros((m,n+1))
   for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j]=a
   if zcol is not None: A[:,n]=zcol
   return A
```
For maxsum, no z col; build separate. We can implement `build_conv_matrix(a, extra_col=None)`.

```
def build_conv_matrix(a, extra_col=None):
   n=a.size; m=2*n-1
   if extra_col is None: A=np.zeros((m,n))
   else: A=np.zeros((m,n+1)); ...; A[:,n]=extra_col
   return A
```

def choose_actual_best(best_holder, top_list, use_eval=True):
    candidates=[]
    if best_holder[0] is not None: candidates.append(best_holder[0])
    for score,a in top_list: candidates.append(a)
    # remove duplicates? Not needed, but may evaluate same. We can avoid by length and maybe sum? no.
    best_a=best_holder[0]; best_actual=float('inf')
    for a in candidates:
       an=normalize_sequence(a)
       s=safe_eval_sequence(an)
       if np.isfinite(s) and s<best_actual:
           best_actual=s; best_a=an
    if best_a is None: best_a=uniform(256)
    return best_a
```

`safe_eval_sequence`:
```
def safe_eval_sequence(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float).copy()
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
   if a.size==0: return inf
   evals=globals().get('evaluate_sequence')
   if callable(evals):
      try:
         val=evals([float(x) for x in a])
         val=float(val)
         if np.isfinite(val): return val
      except Exception: pass
   return sequence_score(a)
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    seed=int(seed); budget=float(budget_s)
    start=time.monotonic()
    if budget <= 20: return [1.0]
    deadline=start+max(20.0,budget-30.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    parent=get_parent_sequence()
    best_holder=[None,float('inf')]
    top_list=[]
    # ensure minimal candidate
    update_best_state(best_holder, top_list, np.ones(256)/256, 'uniform256')
    # lengths
    lengths=[256,512]
    if parent is not None:
       pl=int(parent.size)
       if 64 <= pl <= 1024 and pl not in lengths: lengths.append(pl)
    # if enough time, add 128, 768? maybe 1024
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 1500:
       for l in [128, 1024]:
          if l not in lengths: lengths.append(l)
    lengths=sorted(set(int(l) for l in lengths if 32 <= l <= 2048))
    if not lengths: lengths=[256]
    print("lengths", lengths)
    # Phase 1
    now=time.monotonic()
    phase1_end = min(deadline - 1200, now + min(450.0, (deadline-now)*0.25))
    # Maybe if few lengths, more phase1
    print('Phase1 quick start', phase1_end-now)
    for n in lengths:
       if time.monotonic() > phase1_end: break
       inits=generate_initializations(n,parent,rng)
       inits=inits[:8]
       for idx,(name,a0) in enumerate(inits):
          if time.monotonic() > phase1_end: break
          sub_deadline=min(phase1_end, deadline-1200)
          alloc=max(1.0, sub_deadline-time.monotonic())
          # split
          a_opt,_=mirror_descent(a0, alloc*0.55, eta_init=0.9, top_k=max(16,min(48,n//4)))
          update_best_state(...)
          a_opt2,_=pgd(a_opt, alloc*0.35, lr_init=0.12, top_k=max(16,min(48,n//4)))
          update_best_state(...)
          a_opt3,_=mirror_descent(a_opt2, alloc*0.10, eta_init=0.4, top_k=max(12,min(32,n//4)))
          update_best_state(...)
    # sort top
    if top_list:
       top_list.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    deep_candidates=[]
    for score,a in top_list:
       deep_candidates.append(a)
       if len(deep_candidates)>=8: break
    # ensure parent included if not
    if parent is not None and len(deep_candidates)<8: deep_candidates.append(parent)
    # Phase2 deep LP
    deep_end = min(deadline - 350, time.monotonic() + min(1500.0, (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.60))
    n_deep=max(1,len(deep_candidates))
    for a0 in deep_candidates:
       if time.monotonic() > deep_end: break
       remaining_deep=deep_end-time.monotonic()
       alloc=min(300.0, remaining_deep)
       if alloc < 5: continue
       n=len(a0)
       if n <= 600 and linprog is not None:
           a_opt,best_loc=lp_improve_sequence(normalize_sequence(a0), time_limit=alloc, lp_time=6.0 if n<=300 else 10.0)
       else:
           # gradient pass
           a_opt,_=mirror_descent(a0, alloc*0.5, eta_init=0.7, top_k=max(24,min(64,n//4)))
           a_opt2,_=pgd(a_opt, alloc*0.3, lr_init=0.08, top_k=max(24,min(64,n//4)))
           a_opt=mirror_descent(a_opt2, alloc*0.2, eta_init=0.3, top_k=max(16,min(48,n//4)))
       update_best_state(best_holder, top_list, a_opt, 'deep')
       # sort? not necessary
    # Phase3 local polish on top candidates
    if itemgetter is not None: top_list.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
    polish_end = min(deadline-60, time.monotonic()+min(400.0, (deadline-time.monotonic())*0.5))
    polish_candidates=[]
    # include best and top few under best+0.05
    for score,a in top_list:
       if score <= best_holder[1] + 0.05:
          polish_candidates.append(a)
       if len(polish_candidates)>=4: break
    for a0 in polish_candidates:
       if time.monotonic()>polish_end: break
       alloc=min(150, polish_end-time.monotonic())
       if alloc<3: break
       a_opt=local_polish(a0, time_limit=alloc, rng=rng)
       update_best_state(...)
    # Phase4 final polish best
    if time.monotonic() < deadline-10 and best_holder[0] is not None:
       remaining=deadline-10-time.monotonic()
       if remaining>10:
          a_best=best_holder[0]
          n=len(a_best)
          alloc1=remaining*0.4
          a_opt,_=mirror_descent(a_best, alloc1, eta_init=0.3, top_k=max(16,min(48,n//4)))
          update_best_state(...)
          if time.monotonic() < deadline-5:
             rem2=deadline-5-time.monotonic()
             if rem2>5:
                a_opt,_=pgd(a_best, rem2*0.5, lr_init=0.05...)
                update...
                a_opt=local_polish(a_best, rem2*0.5, rng=rng)
                update...
    # final actual selection
    actual_a=choose_actual_best(best_holder, top_list)
    if actual_a is None: actual_a=np.ones(256)
    actual_a=np.maximum(np.asarray(actual_a,float),0)
    s=actual_a.sum(); s_target=math.sqrt(2.0*actual_a.size)
    if s>0: actual_a=actual_a*(s_target/s)
    # clip 1000, list
    out=[float(x) for x in actual_a]
    print final local/actual? maybe
    return out
```

DD. Let's refine initializations to keep number manageable. Code:
```
def generate_initializations(n,parent,rng):
   inits=[]
   x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
   inits.append(('uniform', np.ones(n)/n))
   if parent is not None:
      p=parent
      xp=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,p.size)
      fp=np.interp(np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n), xp, p)
      inits.append(('parent', normalize_sequence(fp)))
      pu=normalize_sequence(fp); inits.append(('parent_mix', normalize_sequence(0.8*pu+0.2*pu))) # 0.8+0.2=1? need uniform
      uni=np.ones(n)/n
      inits.append(('parent_mix', normalize_sequence(0.8*pu+0.2*uni)))
   for amp in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
      w=1.0+amp*(4.0*(x-0.5)*(x-0.5))
      inits.append(('U'+str(amp), normalize_sequence(w)))
   for amp in (1.0,3.0,8.0):
      w=1.0/np.sqrt(1.0+amp*x)
      inits.append(('L'+str(amp), normalize_sequence(w)))
      w=1.0/np.sqrt(1.0+amp*(1.0-x))
      inits.append(('R'+str(amp), normalize_sequence(w)))
   for amp in (0.5,1.5):
      w=1.0+amp*(1.0-np.sin(np.pi*x))
      inits.append(('edge'+str(amp), normalize_sequence(w)))
   # random smooth
   for r in range(2):
      w=np.ones(n)
      for freq in (1,2,3,5):
          w = w + 0.4*rng.normal(0.0, 1.0/float(freq))*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*x + rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
      w=np.maximum(w,1e-3)
      inits.append(('rand'+str(r), normalize_sequence(w)))
   f=fourier_sqrt_init(n,rng,trials=4)
   if f is not None: inits.append(('foursq', f))
   f=fourier_phase_init(n, 'uniform', steps=15, rng=rng)
   if f is not None: inits.append(('fourph', f))
   return inits
```

EE. `fourier_phase_init` start_mode only 'uniform'. Implement?
```
def fourier_phase_init(n, start_mode='uniform', steps=15, rng=None):
   if n<=0: None
   if start_mode=='uniform': f=np.ones(n)/n
   L=get_fft_size(2*n-1)
   h=np.zeros(L); h[:2*n-1]=1.0/(2*n-1)
   Ht=np.fft.rfft(h,L)
   mht=float(np.max(np.abs(Ht)))
   for _ in range(steps):
      F=np.fft.rfft(f,L)
      Hf=F*F
      absHf=np.abs(Hf)
      ratio=np.ones_like(Ht)
      mask=absHf > 1e-12*max(1.0,mht)
      ratio[mask]=Ht[mask]/Hf[mask]
      Fnew=F*np.sqrt(ratio)
      Fnew[~mask] = 0.05*np.sqrt(Ht[~mask]) + Fnew[~mask]
      g=np.fft.irfft(Fnew,L)[:n]
      g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
      s=g.sum()
      if s<1e-12: break
      f=g/s
      # optional blend with previous: f = 0.7 f + 0.3 g? We are replacing. Could smooth consecutive: keep prev
   return normalize_sequence(f)
```

FF. `fourier_sqrt_init` random signs: Use `F = np.sqrt(np.fft.rfft(h,L).astype(complex))`. To avoid branch issues, after inverse mask. We can try 1 no flips, `trials-1` random.
```
def fourier_sqrt_init(n,rng,trials=4):
   if trials<1: trials=1
   L=...
   Ht=rfft(h,L)
   best=None; best_score=inf
   for trial in range(trials):
      F=np.sqrt(Ht.astype(complex))
      if trial>0:
         for k in range(1, F.size):
             if rng.random() < 0.5: F[k] = -F[k]
      g=irfft(F,L)[:n]
      g=max(g,0); s=sum; if s>... score; if best update
   return best
```

```
def run_linprog(c,A_ub,b_ub,bounds,A_eq=None,b_eq=None,time_limit=8.0):
   if linprog is None: return None
   opts={'time_limit': max(1.0,float(time_limit))}
   try: return linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq, method='highs', options=opts)
   except Exception:
      try: return linprog(c, A_ub=A_ub, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=b_eq)
      except Exception: return None
```

```
def build_conv_matrix(a, extra_col=None):
    n=len(a)
    if n==0:
       m=0
       if extra_col is None: return np.zeros((0,0))
       else: return np.zeros((0,1))
    m=2*n-1
    if extra_col is None: A=np.zeros((m,n))
    else: A=np.zeros((m,n+1))
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j+n,j]=a
    if extra_col is not None:
        A[:,n]=float(extra_col)
    return A
```
For n small, `a` 1D.

Function definitions as planned.

Check `fourier_phase_init`:
```
def fourier_phase_init(n, start_mode="uniform", steps=15, rng=None):
    if n <= 0: return None
    if rng is None: rng = np.random.default_rng(7)
    f = np.ones(n) / float(n)
    L = get_fft_size(2*n - 1)
    h = np.zeros(L)
    h[:2*n-1] = 1.0 / float(2*n-1)
    Ht = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    scale = max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(Ht))))
    for _ in range(int(steps)):
       F = np.fft.rfft(f, L)
       Hf = F * F
       absHf = np.abs(Hf)
       mask = absHf > 1e-12 * scale
       ratio = np.ones_like(Ht)
       ratio[mask] = Ht[mask] / Hf[mask]
       Fnew = F * np.sqrt(ratio)
       if not np.all(mask):
           Fnew[~mask] = F[~mask] + 0.1 * np.sqrt(Ht[~mask])
       g = np.fft.irfft(Fnew, L)[:n]
       g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
       s1 = float(np.sum(np.maximum(g,0)))
       s2 = float(np.sum(np.abs(g)))
       if s1 < 1e-12 and s2 < 1e-12:
           break
       if s1 >= s2:
           f = np.maximum(g,0)/s1
       else:
           f = np.abs(g)/s2
    best = f
    alt = np.abs(f); alt_sum=alt.sum(); if alt_sum>0: alt/=alt_sum; if sequence_score(alt)<sequence_score(f): best=alt
    return normalize_sequence(best)
```
But `sequence_score` uses FFT costly; for each uniqueness? We call once at end. Fine.
In loop, we choose projection each step. Might lose sign information; okay.

LMirror/PGD details code:

```
def mirror_descent(a0, time_limit, eta_init=0.8, top_k=32, max_pat=40):
    if time_limit <= 0: return normalize_sequence(a0), inf
    a = normalize_sequence(a0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-14); a /= a.sum()
    n = a.size
    conv = convolve_full(a)
    obj = 2.0 * float(n) * float(conv.max())
    best = a.copy(); best_obj = obj
    eta = eta_init; patience=0; start=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() - start < time_limit:
        sg = active_subgradient(conv, a, top_k)
        sg_center = sg - float(np.mean(sg))
        scale = float(np.abs(sg_center).max())
        if scale < 1e-18: break
        sg_center /= scale
        accepted = False
        for mult in (1.0, 0.5, 0.25):
            lf = -eta * mult * sg_center
            lf = np.clip(lf, -50.0, 50.0)
            lf -= float(lf.max())
            na = a * np.exp(lf)
            na = np.maximum(na, 1e-15)
            ss = float(na.sum())
            if ss < 1e-15:
                continue
            na /= ss
            nc = convolve_full(na)
            no = 2.0 * float(n) * float(nc.max())
            if no <= obj * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-12:
                a = na; conv = nc; obj = no; accepted = True
                break
        if not accepted:
            patience += 1
            eta *= 0.55
            if patience >= max_pat or eta < 1e-6:
                break
        else:
            if obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
                best_obj = obj; best = a.copy(); patience = 0
            else:
                patience += 1
                if patience >= max_pat:
                    break
    return best, best_obj
```
This may break if no accepted or no best improvement 40. Good.

LP iteration loop:
```
    modes=[]
    if use_linear: modes.append(0)
    if use_maxsum: modes.append(1)
    if not modes: return best,best_obj
    mix_t = mix_values
    it=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic()-start < time_limit:
        M=float(conv.max())
        mode = modes[it % len(modes)]
        base=None
        tl = min(lp_time, max(1.0, time_limit - (time.monotonic()-start)))
        if mode == 0:
            base = lp_linearized_update(a, conv, tl)
        else:
            base = lp_max_sum_update(a, M*(1+1e-8), tl)
        it += 1
        if base is None:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 4: break
            continue
        best_step_obj = obj
        best_step = a
        for mm in mix_values:
            if mm >= 1.0:
                cand = base.copy()
            else:
                cand = (1.0 - float(mm)) * a + float(mm) * base
            cand = normalize_sequence(cand)
            cand_conv = convolve_full(cand)
            cand_obj = 2.0 * n * float(cand_conv.max())
            if cand_obj < best_step_obj - 1e-13:
                best_step_obj = cand_obj; best_step = cand
        if best_step is a:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 4: break
            # break maybe? Try next iteration different mode
            continue
        a = best_step
        conv = convolve_full(a); obj = best_step_obj
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-12:
            best_obj = obj; best = a.copy(); stall=0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall > 5: break
    return best,best_obj
```

`lp_linearized_update` and `lp_max_sum_update` call `run_linprog`. Need ensure they don't take too much; if n small. In `lp_linearized_update`, if `conv` is None, compute. Use `D = np.asarray(conv)`.
```
def lp_linearized_update(a, conv, time_limit_seconds=8.0):
    if linprog is None: return None
    a=normalize_sequence(a); n=a.size
    if n>600: return None
    if conv is None: D=convolve_full(a)
    else: D=conv
    Dmax=float(np.max(D))
    if Dmax <= 0: return a
    A=build_conv_matrix(a, -0.5)
    c=np.zeros(n+1); c[-1]=1.0
    rhs=0.5*np.asarray(D,float)
    zlb=-10.0*Dmax - 1e-8*max(1.0,Dmax)
    zub=10.0*Dmax + 1e-8*max(1.0,Dmax)
    bounds=[(0.0,None) for _ in range(n)]
    bounds.append((zlb,zub))
    res=run_linprog(c,A,rhs,bounds, A_eq=None, b_eq=None, time_limit_seconds=time_limit_seconds)
    x = getattr(res,'x',None) if res is not None else None
    if x is None or len(x) < n+1: return None
    b = np.asarray(x[:n],float)
    b = np.where(np.isfinite(b), b, 0.0)
    b = np.maximum(b,0.0)
    s=float(b.sum())
    if s < 1e-12: return None
    b /= s
    return b
```
But wait equality sum b=1 is not enforced! I forgot! The linearized LP variables b need ∑b=1. We must add equality constraint. For linearized, equality A_eq = [[1...1]] for b, b_eq=[1]. But with z variable, A_eq length n+1 (last 0). Add:
```
Aeq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); Aeq[0,:n]=1.0; beq=np.array([1.0])
```
Pass to run_linprog. Good. For maxsum, no equality.
In constraints, if b unnormalized, z meaningless; equality needed. Add.
For maxsum, objective maximize sum g and no sum equality.

```
def add_to_top_list(score,a,top_list,max_keep=12):
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(score): return
    top_list.append((float(score), normalize_sequence(a).copy()))
    while len(top_list) > int(max_keep):
       worst=0
       for i in range(1,len(top_list)):
          if top_list[i][0] > top_list[worst][0]: worst=i
       del top_list[worst]
```

`safe_eval_sequence`: If global eval returns np.inf? handle.
```
def safe_eval_sequence(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.size==0: return float('inf')
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-300: return float('inf')
    evals=globals().get('evaluate_sequence')
    if callable(evals):
       try:
          val=float(evals([float(x) for x in a]))
          if np.isfinite(val): return val
       except Exception:
          pass
    return sequence_score(a)
```
But if a scaled to sum sqrt(2n), sum>0.01. Good. If eval not available, `sequence_score(a)` normalizes again.

def scale_output(a):

   n=a.size
   target=math.sqrt(2.0*float(n))
   a=a*target
   return np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)

```
def get_parent_sequence():
    p=globals().get('height_sequence_1')
    if p is None: return None
    arr=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
    if arr.ndim !=1: arr=arr.ravel()
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr),arr,0.0)
    arr=np.clip(arr,0.0,1000.0)
    s=arr.sum()
    if s <=1e-12: return None
    return arr/s
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    try: seed=int(seed)
    except: seed=42
    try: budget=float(budget_s)
    except: budget=2400.0
    start=time.monotonic()
    if budget <= 30.0:
       return [float(1.0) for _ in range(64)]  # score? 2*64*1/1? [1]*64 sum64 conv max64? score2. nonempty.
    deadline = start + max(30.0, budget - 20.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    parent=get_parent_sequence()
    best_holder=[None,float('inf')]
    top_list=[]
    update_best_state(best_holder, top_list, np.ones(256)/256.0, 'init')
    lengths=[256,512]
    if parent is not None:
       pl=parent.size
       if 64 <= pl <= 2048 and pl not in lengths: lengths.append(pl)
    if parent is None:
       pass
    T=deadline-start
    phase1_end = start + max(5.0, min(400.0, T*0.18))
    phase2_end = start + max(phase1_end+5.0, min(deadline-start-50.0, T*0.68))
    phase3_end = start + max(phase2_end+5.0, min(deadline-start-5.0, T*0.92))
    phase1_end = min(phase1_end, phase2_end - 2.0)
    phase2_end = min(phase2_end, phase3_end - 2.0)
    phase1_end = max(phase1_end, start)
    # if small, ensure ends <=deadline
    phase1_end = min(phase1_end, deadline)
    phase2_end = min(phase2_end, deadline)
    phase3_end = min(phase3_end, deadline - 1.0)
    if phase1_end < start + 1: phase1_end = start+1
    if phase2_end < phase1_end + 1: phase2_end = phase1_end+1
    if phase3_end < phase2_end + 1: phase3_end = phase2_end+1
```

   target=math.sqrt(2.0*final_a.size)
   final_a = final_a * (target/s)
   final_a = np.clip(final_a,0.0,1000.0)
# Optional final eval? print
try: print('FINAL evaluate:', safe_eval_sequence(final_a)) except: pass
return [float(x) for x in final_a]
```
